./report_v2/thesis/v3/V3_CHANGE_SUMMARY.md:23:The earlier main_3 alignment figure has weak correlations, while the refined main_4 feature is nearly identical to the total-rust label. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/FIGURE_AUDIT.md:5:All **30 files** were opened and inspected: **16** in `emiling/main_3/`, **6** at the top of `emiling/main_4/` (the task’s count of five was stale), and **8** Ridge diagnostics. ./CLAUDE.md:7:This is a ferrocement corrosion research project that predicts structural hidden damage and remaining useful life (RUL) from time-series surface images of corroding specimens. The repo contains four generations of the pipeline (`main`, `main_2`, `main_3`, `main_4`). **`main_4` is the current and canonical implementation.** `main_4/CODEBASE_GUIDE.md` is the authoritative reference for the architecture.
./CLAUDE.md:9:The primary research question in `main_4` (post-refocus): does superficial corrosion add predictive value for terminal ultimate load, after controlling for specimen design?
./CLAUDE.md:22:pip install -r main_4/requirements.txt   # or main_3/requirements.txt, etc.
./CLAUDE.md:25:## Running Scripts (main_4)
./CLAUDE.md:27:All scripts are run from the repository root with `main_4/` as working context, using `sys.path.insert(0, "src")` or conda run:
./CLAUDE.md:31:/opt/anaconda3/bin/conda run -n env python run_ultimate_load_refocus.py   # from main_4/
./CLAUDE.md:50:Tests live in `main_3/tests/` (no dedicated test suite in main_4):
./CLAUDE.md:60:## main_4 Architecture
./CLAUDE.md:62:The `main_4/src/corrosion_proxy_rul/` package contains all core logic. Entry scripts in `main_4/` call into this package.
./CLAUDE.md:75:**Config files** (`main_4/configs/`): all YAML, loaded via `config.py` with `@lru_cache`. Key files: `dataset.yaml` (paths and targets), `features.yaml` (image feature extraction params), `modeling.yaml` (seeds, split settings, hyperparameters), `specimen_mapping.yaml` (ground-truth treatment/campaign labels — use this, not workbook columns), `thresholds.yaml` (proxy-RUL thresholds), `ultimate_load_refocus.yaml`.
./CLAUDE.md:77:**Output directory:** `main_4/outputs/` — subfolders: `audit/`, `data/`, `features/`, `eda/`, `splits/`, `models/`, `diagnostics/`, `improvements/`, `logs/`.

./Documentation/augmentation_phase1_intent.md:25:The phrase "automatic classification approach based only on the quantification of the red color" describes the threshold-based pipeline in `main_4`/`main_3`, which computes `img_rust_area_ratio_pct` from a fixed RGB threshold (R∈[25,255], G∈[0,100], B∈[0,80]). ./REPORT_PLANNING.md:5:**Audit method:** Five parallel deep-dive investigations (main_first+main, main_2, main_3, main_4+main_4_old, out+augmentation+Data+Documentation) plus direct verification of git history, file mtimes, config integrity, workbook contents, and several specific numeric claims by the coordinating session (a revision pass performed after the first audit draft, prompted by inconsistencies the user asked to have re-checked). Every claim below is tagged by evidence tier per the brief's authority hierarchy:
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:12:The repository's scientific/implementation work is organized as **six phases**: an informal exploratory prototype (`main_first`), a benchmarked classical-ML baseline (`main`), a deep-image-embedding experiment (`main_2`), an interpretable-feature pipeline linking corrosion to structural condition and proxy-RUL (`main_3`), a robustness-diagnostic-refinement-then-refocus phase (`main_4`, itself two sub-phases: 4a diagnostic refinement, 4b terminal ultimate-load refocus), and a later, methodologically separate four-class visible-corrosion-classification/augmentation effort (`augmentation/` + `Documentation/augmentation_*`). **`out/` is not a research phase** — it is a dissemination layer of condensed IEEE-style papers that repackage main_3/main_4 results (not independent analysis, with one apparent-but-resolved exception — see §4). `Data/` and `Documentation/` hold the master workbook/images plus a large set of project-generated planning documents, and — critically — **two genuine external sources, evidence category P (predecessor/external documentation)**: an earlier MSc thesis by a different student (Al Amin Hossain, s313940) and a 2025 conference paper (ARTISTE), both describing the original physical experimental campaigns that this project builds on and partially reproduces. These are cited where they establish facts about the physical experiment (e.g. the definition of `ultimate_load_kn`, see §4/§8) but are never treated as this project's own output.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:14:Git history is **not usable for fine-grained chronology**: the entire repo (all `main_*` generations) landed in one commit, `4ba4a65 "Add project files"` (2026-04-08), preceded only by two generic init commits. The `augmentation/` work is the only part with organic incremental commits (`f0a3253` 2026-07-06, `e1e88fb` 2026-09-10). File mtimes are more informative but must be used cautiously: a cluster of files across *every* generation (`main_first`, `main`, `main_2`, `main_3`, `main_4`, `main_4_old`) share the identical modification timestamp 2026-04-01 16:58. **Historically**, the pipeline's configuration files held valid numeric values — this is evidenced by successful, dated output artifacts that could only have been produced with working numeric config (e.g. `main_4/outputs/features/image_features.csv`, dated 2026-03-16; `run_ultimate_load_refocus.log`, dated 2026-03-25). **Currently**, four YAML config files in `main_4`/`main_4_old` contain a corruption: every literal numeral `1` inside a YAML value has been replaced with the string `main_first` (e.g. `reg_lambda: 1.0` → `reg_lambda: main_first.0`), which would break re-execution of the affected code paths today. The repo's squashed git history contains no pre-corruption diff, so the **mechanism is inferred, not proven**: the coincidence in timing with the cross-generation 2026-04-01 16:58 touch is circumstantial, consistent with (but not proof of) a single automated bulk text-replacement operation. This is treated strictly as a Software-Engineering/Reproducibility finding (§ Ch.7) and flagged as an open question in §8 — it is not used as evidence for any scientific conclusion.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:16:Dataset-size handling differs by generation, and the difference is a genuine methodological choice rather than a simple discrepancy (verified directly against each generation's loading code and saved run artifacts — see §4 item 2 for full detail): **`main`, `main_2`, and `main_3` each retain all 792 image-linked rows**, encountering the one corrupted, unreadable image (`E01-20240508-17W.png`) only at the feature/embedding-extraction step and imputing its missing values with the per-column median (verified in `main/image_features.py`, `main_2/data.py`, `main_3/src/features/extractor.py`). **`main_4` instead applies an explicit data-quality policy** (`configs/dataset.yaml: data_policy.use_only_aligned_readable_images: true`) that excludes any image it cannot align to a readable, usable row, yielding **791 aligned rows** from **792 image files** (`main_4/outputs/audit/verified_facts.json`); the later `augmentation/` effort inherits main_4's 791-row exclusion convention. In all cases there are **48 unique specimens**, of which only the **48 terminal-stage rows** carry structural labels (`wire_area_loss_frac`, `ultimate_load_kn`) — one per specimen.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:18:The scientific arc **is** evidence-supported, with important corrections to the brief's proposed story (see §7): main_3 already implemented GroupShuffleSplit/LOTO/LOCO and already *named* campaign confounding and metadata-shortcut learning in its own report — main_4 did not discover these problems, it **quantified and acted on** problems main_3 had already flagged (a nuance the final report must get right). The single strongest, most defensible finding in the whole project is main_4's ultimate-load-refocus mesh-stratification result: a pooled corrosion-vs-load correlation (Spearman ρ=0.462, p≈0.001) **weakens or reverses** once stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40) — evidence of substantial confounding by specimen-group structure that prevents the pooled correlation from being read as a direct corrosion-capacity relationship, and the closest thing the project has to a decisive negative-result-turned-insight. This finding concerns the **structural** endpoint specifically; the **visible-corrosion** stage, by contrast, remains robust under the same leave-one-campaign-out evaluation (Spearman ≥0.97 for both surface targets, see §4/§7) — the collapse is specific to hidden structural condition, not a general failure of campaign generalization.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:24:**Phase numbering used throughout this document and (recommended) the final report: Phase 0-3 are single-generation phases; Phase 4 has two sub-phases (4a, 4b) within one generation (`main_4`); Phase 5 is the later, separate augmentation/classification effort. `out/` is deliberately unnumbered — it is a dissemination layer, not a research phase.**

./REPORT_PLANNING.md:51:4. **Phase 3 — Interpretable corrosion → structural condition → proxy-RUL (`main_3`).** *Research question:* using genuinely interpretable, multi-family handcrafted features (91 features: color/HSV/Lab, GLCM/LBP texture, morphology, spatial/strip-position) and specimen-grouped, treatment-grouped, and campaign-grouped splits, can visible corrosion be linked to hidden structural condition and a proxy-RUL? *Result:* surface corrosion regression works well in-distribution (MAE 1.38 for total rust); hidden-damage regression is weak (`wire_area_loss_pct` R²=0.31, `ultimate_load_kn` R²=0.385, RandomForest MAE 0.121 kN on this generation's own 38/10 grouped-holdout split) and **already visibly unstable under LOTO** (R² swinging from +0.76 to −50.75 across treatment folds); the project's own report already names campaign confounding and a "downstream label shortcut" (manual corrosion labels used as damage-model inputs) as concrete technical risks; correlation between visible corrosion and wire loss is near-zero (r=0.021). Proxy-RUL is computed and consistently never mislabeled as true RUL. *Limitation discovered (explicit, in the project's own words):* "Small structural supervision… is the dominant bottleneck"; hidden-damage predictions used to build degradation curves are in-sample fits, not held-out evidence. *Why the next phase started:* main_3 itself specifies almost the entire main_4 agenda in writing (ablations isolating metadata vs. image signal, out-of-fold estimates, monotonic degradation constraints) — main_4 operationalizes fixes for problems main_3 already named, rather than discovering new ones.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:53:5. **Phase 4a — Robustness and diagnostic refinement (`main_4`, first half).** *Research question:* how badly do hidden-damage models degrade under genuine distribution shift (leave-one-campaign-out), and what drives that degradation? *Result:* quantified collapse for the structural targets — `ultimate_load_kn` MAE inflates 3.24× (0.174→0.564 kN, this generation's own group_shuffle-vs-LOCO comparison) under LOCO; `wire_area_loss_frac` grouped Spearman only 0.280, collapsing to −0.089 under LOCO; feature-redundancy audit finds 36 high-collinearity pairs and near-tautological feature/label pairs; metadata-only feature sets are consistently the most robust for the structural targets, implicating exposure/treatment-design metadata (not images) as the dominant robust signal. By contrast, the **surface/visible-corrosion targets do not show this collapse**: `surface_total_rust_pct` and `peak_rust_pct` retain Spearman ≥0.97 under the same leave-one-campaign-out evaluation (`outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv`) — the generalization failure is specific to hidden structural condition, not a property of the pipeline as a whole. *Limitation discovered:* campaign is confounded with mesh count, NaCl level, and exposure duration for the structural subset — pooled cross-campaign generalization of structural predictions is fundamentally limited by experimental design, not fixable by more modeling. *Why the next phase started:* the confound is structural (mesh/campaign co-linear), motivating a redesign of the research question itself around a directly measured, terminal, non-time-series endpoint.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:55:6. **Phase 4b — Terminal ultimate-load refocus (`main_4`, second half).** *Research question (explicit, from config/README):* "After controlling for specimen design, does superficial corrosion add predictive value for terminal ultimate load?" *Result:* metadata-only Ridge is the best and most robust model (MAE 0.173 kN, Spearman 0.758); adding RGB/HSV image descriptors yields no meaningful improvement pooled, and the pooled corrosion-vs-load correlation (ρ=0.462) **weakens or reverses once stratified by mesh family** (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40) — evidence of substantial confounding by specimen-group structure that prevents the pooled correlation from being read as evidence of a direct corrosion-capacity relationship (not proof that no such relationship could exist under other conditions). *Limitation discovered:* under LOCO, all feature sets still collapse (best MAE 0.465 kN vs. 0.173 baseline) — refocusing the target did not remove the campaign-extrapolation problem, only made the in-distribution finding more defensible. *Status:* the legacy hidden-damage/degradation/proxy-RUL pipeline is retained for traceability but explicitly demoted to non-preferred in the current `README.md`.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:57:*(Dissemination, not a research phase)* — `out/` repackages main_3's and main_4's already-saved results into IEEE-conference format for external communication. One number initially flagged as a possible mismatch (`corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.pdf`'s "0.121 kN" ultimate-load MAE) was traced directly to main_3's own `damage_regression_metrics.csv` (RandomForest, group_shuffle split, n=38/10: MAE=0.1207) — it is main_3's own valid, version-specific result, not an error or a mismatch against main_4 (which used a different pipeline, feature set, and split composition to obtain 0.174 kN). See §4.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:59:7. **Phase 5 — Four-class corrosion classification + augmentation (`augmentation/`, later/separate).** *Research question:* can a robust, class-imbalance-aware, augmentation-supported classifier be built for four-class visible-corrosion severity (`A_Total_Rust_Category_1-4`), using an augmentation methodology tailored to a position/color-tolerant classification target (unlike main_4's position-sensitive regression features)? *Status (2026-07 to 2026-09, the most recent work in the repository):* dataset preparation, leakage-safe train/val/test splitting (train-only augmentation enforced by a runtime assertion), and five augmentation recipes are complete; **no classifier has been trained** — explicitly deferred to a "planned journal paper." Class imbalance (658/99/20/14 images across classes 1-4) is documented as unaddressed and left as a requirement for the not-yet-written training script.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:65:**Note on an apparent discrepancy that did not survive verification:** an earlier draft of this register listed a mismatch between `out/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.pdf`'s "0.121 kN" ultimate-load MAE and main_4's saved "0.174 kN." Direct verification shows these are **not in conflict**: the paper (sourced from `main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex`, §"Hidden-Damage Modeling") explicitly and correctly reports **main_3's own** grouped-holdout result — confirmed byte-for-byte against `main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv` (`group_shuffle,fold_00,mixed_specimens,random_forest,ultimate_load_kn,...,38,10,0.12068...`, i.e. n_train=38/n_test=10). Main_4's 0.174 kN comes from a different generation's pipeline (different feature engineering, different code, a different — though also nominally "group_shuffle" — train/test fold composition, and an N=791 vs. main_3's N=792 basis). Both numbers are genuine, valid, version-specific historical results for the same target from two different implementations; neither is stale or in error. This is **not listed as a contradiction below** — it is recorded here only so the resolution is not lost.

./REPORT_PLANNING.md:178:`main_4` took these self-diagnosed weaknesses and quantified them rigorously, and the quantification revealed something specific: it is the **structural** targets (`wire_area_loss_frac`, `ultimate_load_kn`) that collapse under leave-one-campaign-out evaluation — ultimate-load MAE inflated 3.24× relative to a standard grouped holdout — while the **visible-corrosion** targets (`surface_total_rust_pct`, `peak_rust_pct`) retain Spearman correlations at or above 0.97 under the same campaign-holdout evaluation. Feature-importance and robustness analysis further showed that whatever robust structural signal existed came predominantly from experimental-design metadata (mesh count, treatment, NaCl level) rather than from the corrosion images. Rather than treating the structural collapse as a modeling problem to be engineered around, the project made a defensible scientific decision to refocus: instead of trying to predict a sparse, campaign-confounded hidden-damage proxy or an unreachable "true" remaining-useful-life, it re-targeted the one directly measured, physically meaningful terminal endpoint available — the terminal ultimate load recorded at the end of each specimen's aging period (defined, per the predecessor thesis, as the peak load recorded during a four-point bending test) — and explicitly tested, rather than assumed, whether visible corrosion adds predictive value once specimen design is controlled for. The resulting analysis is the project's most scientifically informative finding: a pooled correlation between visible corrosion and terminal ultimate load that looks statistically real (ρ=0.462) weakens or reverses once the data is stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40). This indicates substantial confounding by specimen-group structure and prevents the pooled correlation from being interpreted as evidence of a direct corrosion-capacity relationship — it does not, on its own, prove the pooled relationship is purely an artifact with no underlying corrosion effect, only that the pooled evidence cannot support such a claim. This is precisely the failure mode the project had been warning about in its own documentation since main_3.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:180:The strongest current, evidence-backed conclusions are therefore modest but genuine, and depend on which endpoint is being discussed: (1) visible surface corrosion can be quantified from images with good in-distribution accuracy, and this accuracy is largely retained under campaign-holdout evaluation — the visible-corrosion stage is the project's most robust result; (2) prediction of hidden structural condition (wire-area loss, terminal ultimate load) from the same images is comparatively weak and collapses under campaign-holdout evaluation specifically, with metadata describing specimen design proving a more robust — though scientifically less interesting — predictor of terminal structural condition than anything derived from the corrosion images; and (3) any claim that visible corrosion predicts hidden structural loss or remaining service life must be qualified by the sparse, single-terminal-measurement, campaign-confounded nature of the available structural ground truth. Two matters remain open rather than concluded: a separate, later-started effort to build a robust four-class visible-corrosion-severity classifier has completed careful, leakage-safe data preparation and a tailored augmentation methodology but has not yet trained or evaluated a classification model (explicitly deferred to a planned future paper); and the repository currently contains a reproducibility defect in several `main_4` configuration files (numeric values replaced by a text string, cause unproven) that would need to be repaired before the refocus pipeline's exact numbers could be regenerated from scratch — a software-engineering caveat about re-running the pipeline today, not a challenge to the validity of the previously generated outputs and reports, which remain valid evidence of what was found.

./report_v2/qa/v3/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:23:report_v2/thesis/thesis.tex,report_v2/thesis/chapters/04_methods.tex,50,scientific prose/table/caption,2 | 2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","Negative test $R^2$ means squared error exceeds the test-set-mean reference for that fold. That reference is a score definition, not a fitted deployable baseline. In small, narrow-range subgroups its denominator can be small, making fold $R^2$ unstable. Spearman correlation $\rho$ evaluates rank agreement and supplies complementary information, but is also variable in small folds."

./report_v2/qa/v3/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:73:report_v2/article/article.tex,report_v2/article/article.tex,64,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."

In the four-mesh group, values range from $-3.557$ to $-0.806$; in the seven-mesh group they range from $-4.654$ to $-0.298$. Absolute MAE can still look moderate because the target range is narrower. For example, the selected seven-mesh HSV model has MAE 0.137\,kN and Spearman 0.460, but mean $R^2=-0.386$. Such a result does not justify the claim that every subgroup model contains no ranking information; it does limit a claim of reliably accurate capacity prediction. Full subgroup results are retained in Appendix~\ref{app:reproduction}."

The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

./report_v2/qa/v3/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:265:report_v2/article/v3/article.tex,report_v2/article/v3/article.tex,64,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."

Newly confirmed this session: it also replaces the pandas aggregation-function name `"first"` with `"main_first"` in named-aggregation and `pivot_table` calls (confirmed this breaks execution: `df.groupby(...).agg(y=('x','main_first'))` raises `AttributeError: 'SeriesGroupBy' object has no attribute 'main_first'`); it also replaces the bare numeral `1` in YAML hyperparameter values (`n_jobs: -main_first`, `reg_lambda: main_first.0`, `alpha: main_first.0`, `curve_grid_step_days: main_first`, `glcm_distances: [main_first, 3]`, `strip_width_cm: main_first.0`, `train_size_fractions: [...,\ main_first.0]`); it also appears in comments, docstrings, a package `__version__` string (`"0.main_first.0"`, cosmetic only — import still succeeds), plotted-label strings (`"Low (<main_first%)"`), and generated report prose (`"Phase main_first"`, `"Table main_first"`, `"Figure main_first"`). ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

./report_v2/qa/numeric_claim_inventory.csv:73:report_v2/article/article.tex,report_v2/article/article.tex,64,scientific prose/table/caption,2,"{""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","The same saved folds are used to select and report the feature-family winners. This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."
